  28. 1p3a post #6: "BQ and past experience deep dive" round (no questions listed); the API and database detail probe came from the system design rounds. https://github.com/FlowSight/coding/blob/main/company%20wise/airbnb/1p3a/6.md
  29. 1p3a post #18: "most proud project, milestone, eng structure, conflict", asked in fine detail. https://github.com/FlowSight/coding/blob/main/company%20wise/airbnb/1p3a/18.md
  30. Haldar et al., "Applying Deep Learning to Airbnb Search", arXiv / KDD 2019 (includes lessons on failed attempts and offline/online gaps). Not fetched. https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.09591
  31. "Experiments at Airbnb", Airbnb Engineering & Data Science (Medium), 2014. Not fetched. https://medium.com/airbnb-engineering/experiments-at-airbnb-e2db3abf39e7
  32. "Beyond A/B Test: Speeding up Airbnb Search Ranking Experimentation through Interleaving", Airbnb Tech Blog (Medium), 2022. Not fetched. https://medium.com/airbnb-engineering/beyond-a-b-test-speeding-up-airbnb-search-ranking-experimentation-through-interleaving-7087afa09c8e
  33. "Life at Airbnb" (core values), Airbnb Careers. Linked from [4]; not fetched. https://careers.airbnb.com/life-at-airbnb/
  34. "Airbnb" company question list (TrueInterview mirror; experiment design prompts including primary and guardrail metrics, a network-aware experiment, causal regression, and LTV and embedding-search ML design; dated entries to September 2026). kevin-2023-code/Tech-Interview-Questions, GitHub, 2026. https://github.com/kevin-2023-code/Tech-Interview-Questions/blob/main/companies/airbnb.md
  35. 1p3a post #16: onsite round 2 "BQ and past experience deep dive"; notes that senior candidates are usually pushed on scaling. FlowSight/coding, GitHub. https://github.com/FlowSight/coding/blob/main/company%20wise/airbnb/1p3a/16.md
  36. 1p3a post #75: a senior manager walked through the candidate's project experience step by step and gave feedback afterwards. https://github.com/FlowSight/coding/blob/main/company%20wise/airbnb/1p3a/75.md
  37. 1p3a post #21: experience round on the candidate's project, mostly trade-offs, conflicts and standard behavioral questions. https://github.com/FlowSight/coding/blob/main/company%20wise/airbnb/1p3a/21.md

08. Core Values Interviews: Champion the Mission, Be a Host, Embrace the Adventure, Be a Cereal Entrepreneur, plus Cross-Functional and Leadership Behavioral Questions

Research note (read first). The shared web-search budget ran out before this chapter was written, so no new search-engine queries were possible. DNS lookups also failed for glassdoor.com, leetcode.com, interviewing.io, hellointerview.com, prepfully.com, igotanoffer.com, medium.com, careers.airbnb.com and news.airbnb.com, and reddit.com refused fetches. The chapter is therefore built from pages that could be read directly on GitHub. These include two TrueInterview-derived Airbnb process guides (one for the MLE track, one for the SWE track), a 2026 company guide that aggregates Hello Interview, interviewing.io, PracHub and LeetCode Discuss, the Tech Interview Handbook's Glassdoor-sourced Airbnb list, and a mirror of more than 100 1point3acres (1p3a) Airbnb interview posts. Every 1p3a post was downloaded and searched for values, behavioral (BQ), hiring-manager (HM) and culture content. Chinese-language posts are paraphrased in English here. Tags: [Reported] means a cited source says a candidate was asked this, or something very close to it, at Airbnb. [Likely] means an inference from the values, the Senior MLE / experimentation role, or Airbnb's products. It does not mean anyone reported it.

Format notes

Aspect What sources report
Number and length One core-values round of about 45 minutes is reported for the MLE track [1]. The SWE track reports one or two rounds of about 45 minutes each [2][3]. Hello Interview's G8 guide (as aggregated) calls it a "30-min mandatory gatekeeper" [4]. Rounds sometimes end early: one 2026 candidate finished in about 20 minutes and still passed [4][10]
Timing Often scheduled after the technical onsite has passed, as a separate gate [4][8][10]. One candidate had it after hiring-manager calls during team matching [18]
Interviewer An employee from any function, outside the hiring team, often a non-engineer [1][2][4][5][7]. One senior candidate's values round was with an engineering manager [15]
What is scored Authentic connection to Airbnb's four values: Champion the Mission, Be a Host, Embrace the Adventure, Be a "Cereal" Entrepreneur [5][12]. Personal stories count as much as work stories [2]. Answers that relabel engineering wins with value names, or that sound rehearsed or framework-driven, land poorly [1][2][3]. Rapport with the interviewer affects the outcome [1]
Weight Scored in the same debrief as the technical rounds [2]. It "can reject you on its own" [4][5]. One candidate failed two behavioral rounds and was told they "answered too quickly, without their own pace" [16]
Related rounds Experience / project deep dive (45-60 min): cross-functional influence, conflict, milestones and "who outside your team you had to align" are probed here. It drives leveling [1][2][17][18][19]. Hiring manager conversation: scope, past projects, team fit, and sometimes BQ questions about missed cross-team ETAs [1][8][20]
Senior bar No source defines a separate senior bar for the values round itself [1][2]. For Senior, scope, cross-team influence and ownership are judged mainly in the deep dive and HM rounds [1][2][20]. Expect values interviewers to probe for the same signals: mentoring, hosting newcomers, and leading through ambiguity [Likely]

Preparation signals from sources: prepare one or two stories per value, including non-work ones such as a cause you support, a time you hosted or built community, and feedback that stung [2][3][4]. Use plain language for a non-engineer interviewer [5]. Pace your answers and do not rush [16].

Questions

A. Opening, background and "Why Airbnb?"

  1. Walk me through your background. [Reported] [8]
    • What thread connects your career moves?
    • What do you do outside work?
  2. Why Airbnb? [Reported] [1][3][4][6][8][11]
    • Why now, and why this team rather than a bigger-name ML org?
    • What specifically drew you to Airbnb as a company, beyond the product? [Reported] [1]
  3. What do you think of Airbnb? [Reported] [6]
    • What does Airbnb do better than any other travel company?
  4. What excites you about the company? [Reported] [6]
  5. Tell me something about yourself and why you would be a good fit. [Reported] [6]
  6. What are your hobbies outside work, or what fun thing from school would you share? [Reported] [8]
  7. Is there anything you really want to do in the future? [Reported] [4]
    • Where do you want to be in five years, and how does Airbnb help?
  8. What is something you do not want to repeat from your last job? [Reported] [6]
  9. What obstacles, challenges or uncertainty do you expect if you join Airbnb? [Reported] [8]
    • What would make you leave Airbnb after a year?

B. Champion the Mission (belonging, community, causes)

  1. What does "belong anywhere" mean to you? [Reported] [6]
    • When have you felt you did not belong, and what changed it?
    • When have you created belonging for someone else?
  2. What part of our mission resonates most with you? [Reported] [6]
  3. How does Airbnb impact our guests and hosts? [Reported] [6]
    • How does it affect the neighborhoods and cities where hosts operate?
  4. What large problems in the world would you solve today? [Reported] [1][6]
    • Why that problem, and what have you personally done about it?
  5. What cause do you actively support, and how do you contribute to it? [Reported] [1][3]
    • How much time do you give it, and what have you learned from it?
  6. When did you last volunteer, and what did you take away from it? [Reported] [8]
  7. How have you contributed to a community, or what volunteer work have you done? [Reported] [4][5]
  8. Tell me a story about your community and the connections you have built. [Reported] [11]
  9. Describe a time you stood up for something you believe in. [Reported] [4]
    • What did it cost you, and would you do it again?
  10. Who has inspired you most, and why? [Reported] [11]
  11. What is Airbnb's hardest problem today, in your view? [Reported] [2]
    • How could ML or experimentation help solve it?
  12. Tell me about a project that reflected your personal or professional values. [Reported] [14]
    • How did the project embody or challenge those values?
  13. Name an organization (not Airbnb) that you admire, and explain why. [Reported] [4] (intern prompt, flagged unverified by the source)
  14. Tell me about a time you chose the right outcome for users even when an internal metric pointed the other way. [Likely]
    • In ML terms: when did you decline to ship a model that won the A/B test?
  15. How would you make sure a ranking or pricing model treats hosts and guests fairly across demographics and markets? [Likely]
    • How would you explain that trade-off to a non-technical interviewer?
  16. How do you keep the people affected by your models (hosts, guests, support agents) in mind when you only see aggregate metrics? [Likely]

C. Be a Host (care for guests, teammates and newcomers)

  1. Give me an example of when you have been a good host. [Reported] [2][6]
    • It does not have to be a listing: an event, a dinner, a visitor, a new hire? [Reported] [11]
  2. Do you have any experience as a host, of any kind? [Reported] [11]
  3. Tell me about your most memorable experience traveling with Airbnb. [Reported] [8]
    • What did the host do that made it memorable?
  4. What are your tips for choosing an Airbnb listing? Have you ever had a bad experience? [Reported] [8]
    • How would you fix that bad experience with product or ML?
  5. Share one of your trips with us. / Tell me about your most memorable trip. [Reported] [4][6]
  6. Name a situation where a company's customer service impressed you. [Reported] [6]
    • What would it take for Airbnb support to match it?
  7. What is the best gift you have ever given or received? [Reported] [6]
  8. If you had an unlimited budget to buy one gift for one person, what would you buy and for whom? [Reported] [6]
  9. Was there a senior colleague who helped you a lot early in your career? [Reported] [8]
    • How have you paid that forward?
  10. Tell me about a time you made a new teammate feel welcome and productive. [Likely]
    • What did you change in onboarding docs, tooling or pairing as a result?
  11. Describe a time you mentored an engineer or scientist to a measurable improvement. [Likely]
    • How did you adapt your approach to their style?
  12. Tell me about a time you went out of your way for an internal customer, such as a data scientist or product team that consumed your model or platform. [Likely]
  13. Tell me about a time you supported a teammate going through a hard period. [Likely]
  14. How do you make people who disagree with you feel heard in a design review or experiment readout? [Likely]
  15. Tell me about a time you had to give someone terrible news. [Reported] [5][6]
    • How did you prepare, and how did they react?

D. Embrace the Adventure (curiosity, risk, discomfort, resilience)

  1. What adventurous moments or decisions have you had in life? How do you take risks? [Reported] [8]
  2. Tell me about a calculated risk you took. [Reported] [2]
    • How did you size the downside before you committed?
  3. Tell me about a time you were uncomfortable and how you dealt with it. [Reported] [5][6]
  4. If you had an unlimited budget and could go anywhere, where would you go? [Reported] [6]
  5. What is something new you can teach your interviewer in a few minutes? [Reported] [6]
  6. Tell me about a self-learning experience: how you learned something, and whether it helped your later work. [Reported] [13]
  7. What is the most challenging project, in or out of work, that you have done recently? [Reported] [6][11]
  8. How do you manage the gap between expectations and reality, at work or in life? [Reported] [8]
  9. Tell me about a time a major change you did not choose (reorg, layoff, pivot) hit your team. How did you respond? [Likely]
  10. Tell me about a time you worked in a domain you knew nothing about, for example moving from recommendation to causal inference. [Likely]
    • How quickly were you useful, and what did you get wrong first?
  11. Tell me about an experiment or model idea that failed. What did you learn, and what did you do next? [Likely]
    • How did you tell the team?
  12. When have you changed your mind because of data that contradicted a strong belief? [Likely]
  13. If you were a gerbil, which gerbil would you be? (a reported curveball used to test spontaneity) [Reported] [6]

E. Be a "Cereal" Entrepreneur (resourcefulness, scrappiness, creativity)

  1. Tell me about a time you were scrappy and built something valuable with very limited resources. [Likely] [5][12]
    • What did you deliberately leave out?
  2. What is something you would like to remove from the Airbnb experience? [Reported] [5][6]
    • How would you test whether removing it helps?
  3. Tell me about a time you saw an unowned problem and took it on without being asked. [Likely]
  4. Describe a time you shipped a quick heuristic or a small experiment instead of waiting for the perfect model. [Likely]
    • How did you know when it was time to replace it?
  5. Tell me about a time you found a creative workaround for a data, compute or labeling constraint. [Likely]
  6. Have you ever started something of your own, such as a side project, club, startup or open-source tool? [Likely]
    • What did it teach you about customers?
  7. Tell me about a time you turned a one-off analysis or script into a tool other teams adopted. [Likely]
  8. Tell me about a time you reduced cost (infrastructure, experiment duration, labeling spend) without hurting quality. [Likely]
    • Which metrics showed it, and who signed off?
  9. Pitch me one product or ML idea for Airbnb that nobody has asked you to build. [Likely]
    • How would you validate it in two weeks?

F. Feedback, failure and self-reflection

  1. Have you given or received hard feedback? Tell me about it. [Reported] [2][3][11]
    • What did you change afterwards, and how would the other person describe it?
  2. Have you received negative feedback from others, and how did you handle it? [Reported] [13]
  3. Tell me about a time you did not meet expectations, and how you improved using customer feedback. [Reported] [13]
  4. What could you have done better in a past experience? [Reported] [8]
  5. What was your most difficult task, and how did you get through it? [Reported] [11]
  6. Tell me about a time you failed. [Likely] [3]
    • What would you do differently, and what did you change in your process?
  7. What would your last manager say is your biggest area for growth? [Likely]
  8. Tell me about a time you made a mistake that affected users or a launch, such as a bad model push or a wrong experiment readout. [Likely]
    • How did you communicate it, and what guardrail did you add?

G. Cross-functional collaboration and communication

  1. Give an example of communicating with your own team or with a different team. [Reported] [8]
  2. How do you make sure a design lands well with teams in different time zones or cultural backgrounds? What problems came up? [Reported] [8]
  3. In an urgent situation, how do you make sure you get time with senior people? [Reported] [8]
  4. How did you work with senior management on large projects and with multiple internal teams? [Reported] [6]
  5. Tell me about a time another team's internal ETA slipped and affected you, so the timeline moved or scope shrank. [Reported] [8]
    • How did you identify the root cause? [Reported] [8]
    • How was work split across the team? [Reported] [8]
    • Did you have a backup plan to unblock yourself? [Reported] [8]
  6. Show me a project you are proud of that crossed organizations or functions. [Reported] [17]
    • How were milestones set, how was engineering structured, and what conflicts arose? [Reported] [17]
  7. Tell me about a cross-team collaboration challenge on a past project. [Reported] [18]
  8. Tell me about a conflict, failure, tight deadline or complex project you owned. [Reported] [4]
  9. How did you set milestones on your project, and how did you ensure system quality and user benefit after deployment? [Reported] [19]
    • What would you change if you redesigned it? [Reported] [19]
  10. Tell me about a time you disagreed with a product manager about the success metric for an experiment. [Likely]
    • Whose metric won, and why?
  11. Tell me about a time a data scientist and you read the same A/B test differently. How was it resolved? [Likely]
  12. How do you explain a model's behavior or an experiment's uncertainty to a non-technical stakeholder, such as an operations or policy partner? [Likely]
    • Explain a confidence interval to me as if I were your values interviewer.
  13. Tell me about a time you influenced a decision with no formal authority over the people making it. [Likely]
  14. Tell me about a time you had to say no to a partner team's request. [Likely]
  15. How do you work with designers and PMs when a model's output changes the user experience, for example in ranking or pricing suggestions? [Likely]
  16. Describe a time you resolved friction between two teammates or two teams. [Likely]

H. Senior-level ownership and leadership (MLE and experimentation flavor)

  1. What is the biggest obstacle in moving from writing code to owning an entire project? [Reported] [8]
  2. What is your current scope, and what scope do you expect here? [Reported] [20]
    • Do you want to be a tech lead, or a strong IC on a team that already has one?
  3. What was the hardest technical or organizational challenge you faced? [Reported] [20]
  4. Tell me about a time you set technical direction for a group larger than your own team. [Likely]
  5. Tell me about a time you stopped a launch, or argued against shipping, because the experiment evidence was weak. [Likely]
    • How did leadership react, and what happened next?
  6. Tell me about a time you raised the bar for experimentation rigor on your team, for example with pre-registration, guardrails or a review process. [Likely]
  7. How do you balance short-term booking or revenue metrics against long-term guest and host trust? [Likely]
  8. Tell me about a time you had to deliver an inconclusive or negative result to a team that badly wanted a win. [Likely]
  9. How do you grow other engineers' judgment about when ML is the wrong tool? [Likely]
  10. Tell me about a time you made a decision with incomplete data under time pressure. [Likely]
  11. How do you prioritize when three teams all need your help this quarter? [Likely]
  12. Tell me about a time you handed off something you built so that it no longer depended on you. [Likely]

I. Hiring-manager conversation (behavioral portion)

  1. Walk me through your most interesting project. What were the key design points and challenges? [Reported] [20][21]
  2. Which problems on our roadmap map onto your background, and where would you need to ramp up? [Reported] [1]
  3. Who outside your team did you have to align on your biggest project, and how? [Reported] [1][2]
  4. How did you scope work when requirements changed mid-project? [Reported] [2]
  5. Why did you choose a long-term plan over a short-term fix, and how did you justify it? [Reported] [20]
  6. Which metrics did you add yourself, and which existed already? [Reported] [20]
  7. What do you need from a manager to do your best work? [Likely]
  8. What kind of team culture do you create when you are the most senior IC in the room? [Likely]

09. Airbnb ML Context: Papers, Platforms and Recent AI Products as Interview Questions

This chapter turns Airbnb's public ML record into questions you could be asked. That record covers the KDD/CIKM papers, the Bighead / Zipline / Chronon platform line, Minerva and ERF, interleaving, and the 2024-2026 LLM customer-support and AI-search launches. The chapter has no round of its own. Its questions come up inside the ML system design rounds, the experience deep dive (past-project round) and the experimentation discussion. They work best as design prompts ("design X at Airbnb"), so the system-design framing is kept throughout.

Research note (read first). This chapter went through an adversarial review on 2026-10-14. The shared web-search budget was used up, so no new search queries could run. The proxy could not resolve prachub.com, teamblind.com, 1point3acres.com, interviewquery.com, trueinterview.io, arxiv.org, medium.com, airbnb.tech, infoq.com, cxtoday.com, zenml.io, altexsoft.com or syncedreview.com. Twelve of the previously cited URLs were tried and all failed to resolve. GitHub was the only reachable host.

  • Fetched and read on 2026-10-14: [1]-[7]. These are the TrueInterview-derived Airbnb MLE guide (3 candidate reports, mostly 2026 Q1-Q2), the dated Airbnb question index (entries to Sep 2026), the general Airbnb process page, two 1point3acres MLE reports mirrored on GitHub, the Chronon repository, and the applied-ml index.
  • URL confirmed: [8]-[27]. Title, URL and year match the applied-ml index [7], but the page itself was not opened.
  • Unverified: [28]-[54]. These are carried over from the previous version of this chapter and could not be opened. Check them before you rely on them.

Tags. [Reported] means a fetched source [1]-[5] says a candidate was asked this at Airbnb, or something very close to it. [Likely] means an inference from Airbnb's published work, the role and the senior bar. A citation on a [Likely] question only gives context.

Retagging in this review. Six questions were moved from [Reported] to [Likely] because their only sources (PracHub, Blind, CleverPrep) could not be opened: FM vs. DNN, offline-NDCG-up-but-A/B-flat, precision/recall/latency/cost, host fairness, the "family friendly" classifier and the ML-platform code review. The topics are plausible but unconfirmed.

Format notes (how this material surfaces in the loop)

  • Where it shows up: Airbnb MLE loops have one or two ML system design rounds, sometimes more. The prompts are open-ended marketplace problems with no code: search ranking, a property's lifetime value, recommendations, relisting detection, cover photos [1][3]. The loop is decided mainly in these rounds, and a borderline design signal can earn an extra design round [1].
  • Experience round can turn into design: One MLE candidate expected a past-project deep dive in the "ML experience" round, but the interviewer asked an unplanned ML design question instead [5]. Prepare to whiteboard an Airbnb problem in any ML round.
  • Senior bar: At Senior (G9) and above, design carries more weight. In the reports, rejection feedback pointed to design depth or overall match, never to coding [1]. One ML rejection cited "jumping straight to model ideas"; the guide's advice is "Problem and label before model" [3].
  • Recent senior loops: These loops added ranking and retrieval prompts around generative-AI customer-support tools and a document-redaction exercise [1]. In design rounds, interviewers push on candidate generation, latency, freshness, cold start for new listings and monitoring [1].
  • Interviewer steering: Treat a redirection as a hint and restate the narrower scope before going on [1].
  • Unverified prep advice: This advice comes from search-index excerpts of Blind and PracHub [52][53] and could not be re-checked: "be very familiar with Airbnb business and product," because questions often reflect a real problem the team is working on.
  • How to use this chapter: You will rarely be asked to recite a paper. You are more likely to get the paper's problem as an open design prompt, then a probe on whether you know how Airbnb approached it.

1. Search ranking at Airbnb (KDD 2019 / KDD 2020 deep learning line)

1.1 Search-ranking prompts

  1. [Reported] Rank home search results when the guest gives no text query, only location, dates and guest count. Reported Sep 2026. [1][2][3]
    • What is the label: click, wishlist, booking request, or an uncancelled booking that the host accepted?
    • How do you handle the fact that most of a guest's searches never end in a booking?
    • What retrieval stage feeds the ranker, and how large is the candidate set?
    • Walk through candidate generation, offline evaluation, online evaluation and deployment. [1]
  2. [Reported] Design a personalized search ranking system for Airbnb. [1][2]
    • [Likely] How does ranking Experiences differ from ranking Homes (smaller inventory, time-slot availability, cold start)? [11]
    • How would you surface new or high-quality Experiences that have little engagement history?
  3. [Reported] In your ranking design, the interviewer pushes on latency, feature freshness, cold start for new listings and monitoring. Which of these sets your architecture, and what would you give up first? [1]
  4. [Likely] In Airbnb search ranking, how do you balance relevance, personalization and host fairness?
    • How would you measure fairness or exposure for hosts, and which guardrail would you set on it?
  5. [Likely] Compare a factorization machine with an embedding + DNN ranker for this problem. Which would you ship, and why? [52]
    • Explain the tradeoffs in interaction modeling, serving latency and debuggability.
  6. [Likely] Your new ranker improves offline NDCG, but the online A/B test is flat or negative. What do you do? [1][52]
    • Possible causes include position bias in training logs, label leakage, distribution shift, a metric mismatch with bookings, and set-level effects.
  7. [Likely] How do you trade off precision, recall, latency and infrastructure cost in a production ranking system? [52]

1.2 Moving from GBDT to neural ranking ("Applying Deep Learning to Airbnb Search")

  1. [Likely] Airbnb's GBDT search ranker plateaued, and the team moved to neural networks [8]. When is that a good decision, and how would you de-risk it?
    • Why start with a single-hidden-layer network to validate the pipeline before trying deeper models?
    • Which feature-engineering habits from GBDTs (raw counts, unnormalized prices) hurt neural nets, and how do you fix them?
  2. [Likely] Airbnb reported that listing-ID embeddings overfit badly in search ranking [8]. Why would listing IDs overfit in a marketplace where each listing books only a few times a year?
    • What features would you use in place of high-cardinality IDs?
  3. [Likely] A listing can be booked by only one guest per night. How would you build a "booking probability" label from search logs under that constraint?
    • How do you deal with listings that were unavailable, or that the host rejected?
  4. [Likely] How do you check whether a neural ranker learned the expected relationship with price, for example a preference for cheaper listings when everything else is equal?
    • What would you do if partial-dependence plots showed the model preferring more expensive listings?
  5. [Likely] Airbnb search is a two-sided marketplace. How would you include host preferences (acceptance, rejection, Instant Book) in the ranking objective?

1.3 Position bias, cold start and architecture ("Improving Deep Learning for Airbnb Search")

  1. [Likely] Explain how you would correct for position bias in training data from search logs. [9]
    • How does a shallow position "tower" whose logit is added to the relevance tower work, and how do you serve without it?
    • What assumption about factorizing click probability does this rely on?
    • When would you use dropout on the position feature instead?
  2. [Likely] New Airbnb listings have no engagement history. How do you rank them fairly without flooding results with untested inventory? [9]
    • How would you predict engagement features for a new listing from its attributes and nearby listings?
    • How do you A/B test a cold-start change when its effects show up on the supply side?
  3. [Likely] Airbnb found that a deeper or more complex network did not automatically win [9]. How do you decide when to stop increasing model capacity?
  4. [Likely] How would you design a two-tower "query/guest" vs "listing" architecture for ranking, and what is lost compared with a full cross-feature network?

1.4 Diversity and set-level ranking

  1. [Likely] Ranking each listing by its own booking probability ignores how similar the listings are to each other. How would you diversify Airbnb search results? [12][13]
    • How do you define similarity: price, location, room type, or a learned embedding?
    • Why might diversity optimization break interleaving-based evaluation? [18][37]
  2. [Likely] How does a set-level (listwise or contextual) ranker differ from a pointwise one, and how would you serve it within latency budgets?

1.5 Multi-task and multi-objective ranking (Journey Ranker, KDD 2023; distillation, KDD 2024)

  1. [Likely] Guests research for days or weeks before booking, and hosts can reject or cancel. How would you design a ranker that uses intermediate "milestones" such as clicks, wishlists, contact host and booking request? [28]
    • How do you treat negative milestones such as host rejection or guest cancellation?
    • How would you use guest state (early vs late in the journey) and query context as inputs?
    • How would you make the architecture modular so that other surfaces (email, Experiences, recommendations) can reuse it?
  2. [Likely] Search ranking must maximize bookings while keeping cancellations, low reviews and customer-support contacts down. How would you combine these objectives? [29]
    • What are the drawbacks of a hand-tuned weighted sum of task heads?
    • How would distilling several single-objective "teacher" models into one "student" ranker work?
    • How do you add a non-differentiable business objective?
    • How do you show that the secondary-objective constraints still hold after launch?
  3. [Likely] How would you choose the primary metric for a multi-task ranker: uncancelled bookings, booking value, or something else?

1.6 Maps, home page and split stays

  1. [Likely] On a map there is no ranked list, and attention does not decay by rank position. How would you evaluate and train ranking for map pins? [30]
    • Why is NDCG a poor fit for maps, and what attention model (uniform, tiered pins, spatial decay) would you use?
    • How would you decide which listings get full price pins and which get mini-pins?
  2. [Likely] How should list ranking and map results stay consistent when a guest pans or zooms?
  3. [Reported] Design the Airbnb home page: search, availability and ranking working together. Reported Mar 2026. [2]
    • Where does availability filtering happen relative to retrieval and ranking, and how stale can availability be?
    • How do you rank home-page modules or carousels against each other, not only listings within a module?
  4. [Likely] When no single listing is free for all of a guest's dates, Airbnb can suggest a split stay across two listings. How would you rank listing pairs for that? A split-stay combination problem appears in the coding pool [2].
    • How do you score a pair (distance between the listings, combined price, quality of each) and keep the candidate space tractable?

2. Retrieval, location and semantic search

  1. [Likely] Design the first embedding-based retrieval (EBR) stage for Airbnb search, which narrows eligible homes before the expensive ranker runs. [31]
    • How do you build training pairs from search sessions, and which negatives do you use (random, same-market, or hard negatives from booked-vs-viewed)?
    • Why compute the listing tower offline daily and the query tower online?
    • How would you choose an ANN index (IVF vs HNSW) given that availability and price filters change constantly?
    • How do you combine EBR with hard filters such as dates, guest count and price?
  2. [Likely] Airbnb reported that EBR increased bookings and also cut compute [31]. How could a retrieval change save compute and improve conversion at the same time?
  3. [Reported] Design semantic search over user embeddings. Reported Apr 2026. [2]
    • What does a user embedding encode (stays, searches, wishlists, reviews), and how is it refreshed?
    • What is the query: a free-text description, another user, or a listing? How does that change the index design?
    • How do you evaluate semantic relevance when there is no clean labeled set?
  4. [Likely] For a query like "Lake Tahoe," which geographic areas should search retrieve from? How would you move from heuristic bounding boxes to a learned approach? [32]
    • How do you evaluate location recall, meaning whether the area included the listing the guest eventually booked?
    • Why might you use reinforcement learning or bandit exploration for location retrieval?
  5. [Likely] Suppose you represent the world as discrete location cells for retrieval. How would you do it, and what tradeoff do you face between cell size, precision and recall? [32]
  6. [Likely] How would you support flexible-date or "anywhere" searches, where there is no fixed location or date?

3. Embeddings, content understanding and recommendations

  1. [Likely] Walk through how you would learn listing embeddings from click sessions with a skip-gram style model. [10][33]
    • Why use the booked listing as a global context in every session window?
    • Why sample negatives from the same market as well as at random?
    • How would you check embedding quality offline (same-market neighbors, similar price, similar listing type)?
  2. [Likely] Bookings are too sparse per user and per listing to learn ID embeddings directly. How did Airbnb's user-type and listing-type embeddings address this, and how would you build them? [10]
    • How do you put host rejections into the embedding space?
  3. [Reported] Design Airbnb listing recommendation. [5]
    • [Likely] Make it concrete as the "similar listings" carousel on a listing page [10][33]. What offline metric and what online A/B metric would you use?
    • How would you handle a new listing with no clicks?
  4. [Likely] How would you use short-term (in-session) and long-term (past bookings) interest embeddings as real-time ranking features?
    • What latency and freshness requirements apply, and where do the session features come from?
  5. [Likely] Airbnb trained a two-tower model on guest engagement to produce listing embeddings for host-side and similar-listing surfaces [33]. How do you correct sampling bias in in-batch negatives?
  6. [Likely] How would you build one multimodal embedding per listing from many photos and text fields, without just averaging them? [34]
    • How would you use LLMs or CLIP-style models to align text and images?
    • How would you compress the embedding (PCA, quantization) to fit a serving memory budget?
    • How does this embedding help with zero-shot free-text search and cold-start listings?
  7. [Likely] Design a room-type classifier for listing photos (bedroom, kitchen, bathroom) and its labeling pipeline. Airbnb's Photo Tour depends on this kind of classification. [20]
    • How would multi-task learning, ensembling or distillation improve accuracy within latency limits?
  8. [Reported] Choose a listing's cover photo so that it raises bookings, including for new listings with no engagement data. [1]
    • Do you predict a photo-quality score, run a bandit over candidate photos, or rank photos by expected click-through? What is the label?
    • How do you avoid optimizing for click-bait photos that lead to worse reviews?
    • How would you A/B test this when the treatment changes what other guests see in search?
  9. [Likely] Design amenity detection from listing photos, and use it to check that listings really have the amenities they claim. [21]
    • How do you label at scale, and what precision do you need before showing a detected amenity to guests?
  10. [Likely] Design Airbnb Categories (collections such as "Treehouses" or "Amazing pools"), where ML proposes listings and humans review them. [24]
    • How do you choose which candidates go to human review, and how do reviewer decisions retrain the model?
    • How do you rank inside a category versus across categories?
  11. [Likely] How would you build a knowledge graph of places, Experiences and listing attributes, and use it in search or recommendations? [23]

4. Listing quality, pricing, value and trust models

  1. [Reported] Design a system that finds high-quality listings (asked at the phone screen in one MLE loop). [1]
    • How do you define "quality" without circular reliance on bookings: reviews, host behavior, photos, cancellations?
    • How would the score feed search, badges or curated collections?
  2. [Reported] Design a model that predicts or recommends the nightly price for an Airbnb listing, helping hosts set prices from market demand. [4][5]
    • Listings are not identical products, so how do you estimate demand without a clean demand curve? [35]
    • How would a "booking probability, then price strategy" two-stage design work, and why might you use a custom loss with an asymmetric penalty instead of MSE? [35]
    • How do you evaluate price suggestions when you never see what would have happened at a different price?
    • How do you handle hosts who do not accept the suggestion (selection bias in adoption)?
  3. [Reported] Estimate a property's lifetime value. First agree with the interviewer on what lifetime value means here. Reported May 2026. [1][2][3]
    • Would you frame it as regression or as bucketized classification, and why might bucketizing be the better choice for steering decisions? [1]
    • What horizon and discounting do you use, and how do you handle censoring for new listings? [25]
    • Which teams would consume the estimate (host acquisition, onboarding, support priority), and how would you check that it changes their decisions?
  4. [Reported] Design a system that calculates customer (guest) lifetime value and builds its ML pipeline. [4]
    • How does guest LTV differ from listing LTV in labels, horizon and use?
    • How would you use it to pick users for a promotional campaign and measure incremental impact? [32]
  5. [Reported] Design relisting detection: catch a host who deletes a listing and relists the same property, for example to shed bad reviews. Show how the outputs show up in online metrics. [1][2][3]
    • How would geohashing help generate candidate pairs, and how do you handle nearby but distinct units in the same building? [1]
    • Which signals would you combine (photo embeddings, text similarity, host/device/payment links)?
    • What action follows a match (block, merge reviews, manual review), and how do you measure false positives?
  6. [Likely] Design a "family friendly" listing classifier. [52]
    • Where do the labels come from (amenities, reviews, host text, photos), and how noisy are they?
    • How would you use an LLM or vision-language model to bootstrap labels, and how do you audit it?
  7. [Likely] Design a model that predicts guest or host cancellations at booking time.
    • How would that prediction be used: in ranking, in host tools, or in support?
  8. [Likely] Design a real-time booking-time risk model for offline risks such as property damage or unauthorized parties. [36]
    • How do you set the decision threshold when false positives block legitimate guests?
    • How do you get labels when most incidents are never reported?
    • How do you measure the impact of a risk model in an A/B test without letting fraud through in control?
  9. [Likely] Design a system that detects fake listings or scam messages using graph signals, embeddings and device IDs. [36]
  10. [Likely] How would a guest-host match quality model feed into both search ranking and Instant Book eligibility?
  11. [Likely] Forecast vacant nights or occupancy for each listing so that host tools can suggest price or availability changes. "Average Vacant Days" appears as an Airbnb SQL prompt [2].
    • How do you handle listings the host blocks on purpose, which look the same as nights with no demand?

5. ML platform: Bighead, Zipline, Chronon

5.1 Bighead (end-to-end ML platform)

  1. [Likely] Airbnb built Bighead because shipping a model took weeks to months [38]. If you joined a team without a unified ML platform, which component would you build first, and why?
    • How would you standardize the path from notebook to production?
    • How do you make training reproducible: environment pinning with Docker, data snapshots, versioned features?
  2. [Likely] How would you design model serving for a search ranker that scores hundreds of listings per request under a strict p99 latency budget?
    • What do you batch, cache or precompute?
    • How do you safely roll out a new model version (shadow, canary, A/B)?
  3. [Likely] What should a production ML monitoring stack watch for an Airbnb ranker (feature drift, prediction drift, calibration, business KPIs)? [1]
  4. [Likely] How would you review an ML pipeline pull request for correctness and training/serving consistency? A multi-PR code-review round is reported in Airbnb SWE loops (Jun 2026) [2], and one unverified post describes it for an ML-platform role [54].

5.2 Zipline and Chronon (feature platform)

  1. [Likely] What is training/serving skew, and how does a "define once, compute offline and online" feature platform such as Chronon prevent it? Airbnb postings stress training/serving skew [1]. [6][19]
    • What does point-in-time correctness mean for a feature like "number of views of this listing in the last 7 days"?
    • What goes wrong if you join today's snapshot to last month's labels?
  2. [Likely] Explain how a Chronon GroupBy (windowed aggregations such as SUM, COUNT, AVERAGE keyed by an entity) and a Join (a left event source plus several GroupBys) produce a point-in-time training set. [6]
    • How do you backfill a new feature over a year of history efficiently, without snapshotting every timestamp? [6][19]
    • How do batch and streaming paths combine to serve a fresh value online?
  3. [Likely] Chronon measures online/offline consistency by logging online fetches, replaying them as a backfill and comparing the values [6]. How would you set alert thresholds on that metric, and what would you do when it drifts?
  4. [Likely] Design the real-time feature pipeline that supplies "listings this guest viewed in the current session" to the search ranker within a few hundred milliseconds.
    • Which store (key-value store, in-memory cache) do you use, and what happens on a cache miss?
  5. [Likely] Chronon is open source, and its repository lists other companies such as Stripe among its adopters [6]. What tradeoffs would you weigh between an in-house feature platform and an off-the-shelf feature store?
  6. [Likely] How would you reuse a feature platform for LLM applications, for example to fetch real-time user and reservation context for a support agent? [39]

6. Metrics and experimentation context: Minerva, ERF, interleaving

  1. [Likely] Airbnb built Minerva so that a metric is defined once and used for analytics, experimentation and reporting [40][41]. Why does that matter for ML launch decisions?
    • What goes wrong when Data Science and Finance compute "bookings" with different logic?
    • How would you add a new metric for your model launch without breaking downstream consumers?
  2. [Reported] Design the data model behind cumulative booking metrics, including each user's first and last booking times. Reported Sep 2026. [2]
    • [Likely] How would this metric layer serve both experiment readouts and dashboards consistently (the Minerva problem)? [40]
  3. [Reported] Design an A/B testing platform. Reported Apr 2026. [2]
    • [Likely] Using Airbnb's ERF as the reference [14], how do you join assignment logs with metric events and compute results for thousands of metric/experiment pairs?
    • At what unit do you randomize for a search ranker: user, device or search session? What happens with logged-out users?
    • How do you deal with users who switch devices between searching and booking?
  4. [Reported] Design robust primary and guardrail metrics for an experiment. [2]
    • [Likely] Airbnb escalates a launch when it moves another team's metrics, for example a test that raised bookings but lowered review ratings [15]. Which guardrails would you set for a ranking launch: cancellations, review ratings, CS contacts, host earnings, latency?
    • How do you set an escalation threshold, and what is a "power guardrail"? [15]
  5. [Reported] Design an A/B test that uses causal inference. [2]
    • [Likely] When you cannot randomize (a policy change in one market, a host-side launch), how would an ML-based counterfactual estimator such as Airbnb's ACE work, and how do you check it for bias? [17]
  6. [Reported] Design an experiment for a listing badge (for example a "fast Wi-Fi" badge) that accounts for network effects. [2]
    • Why does a listing-level badge violate SUTVA when guests compare badged and unbadged listings in the same search?
    • Would you randomize by listing, by guest, by market, or with a two-sided (bipartite) design? What bias does each leave?
  7. [Likely] Bookings have high variance and long latency. How would you make ranking experiments more sensitive? [42]
    • How could a model of leading indicators (a surrogate or value function) give credit for the eventual booking to earlier steps?
    • What are the risks of optimizing a surrogate metric instead of bookings?
  8. [Likely] Airbnb puts a monetary "future value" on actions so that teams can compare tradeoffs on a common scale [16]. How would you estimate the long-term value of a cancellation, a bad review or a support contact, and use it in a launch decision?
  9. [Likely] Airbnb uses interleaving to screen search-ranking candidates on a small share of traffic before A/B testing [18][37]. Explain team-draft interleaving and how you would attribute bookings to one ranker.
    • When is interleaving invalid (set-level diversity, results shared with the map)?
    • What is online counterfactual evaluation, and when would you use it instead?
    • How do you show that interleaving outcomes predict A/B outcomes?
  10. [Likely] In a two-sided marketplace, treatment-group and control-group guests compete for the same listing-nights, so a ranking test can move bookings from control to treatment. How do you detect and correct this interference?
    • When would you use market-level or time-split (switchback) randomization?
  11. [Likely] A model launch helps guests but lowers host earnings in some markets. How do you decide whether to ship?

7. 2024-2026 Airbnb AI: customer-service LLMs

  1. [Reported] Design retrieval and ranking for a generative-AI tool that helps customer-support staff answer guest and host issues (recent senior loops). [1]
    • What does the knowledge base contain (policies, help articles, past resolved tickets), and how do you chunk and index it?
    • How do you evaluate retrieval quality and answer faithfulness before agents see the answers?
    • How do you stop the system from citing an outdated or region-specific policy?
  2. [Reported] Generate customer-support instructions from company policies. Reported Sep 2026 in senior loops. [1][2]
    • [Likely] How would you turn policies and workflows into a format an LLM can follow reliably, such as an intent-context-action structure? [46]
    • How would you generate synthetic fine-tuning or evaluation data from those instructions?
    • How do you detect when a policy change makes generated instructions stale?
  3. [Reported] Redact sensitive information (PII, contact details, payment data) from documents or messages. In recent senior loops this was reported as a coding exercise; here it is asked as a design question. [1]
    • How do you trade off recall (missing PII) against precision (over-redacting useful content)?
    • Would you use regex, NER, an LLM, or a cascade, and why? How does Airbnb-scale automated data classification inform this? [27]
  4. [Reported] Design a customer-support ticketing and routing system. Reported Jun 2026 as a system design prompt. [2]
    • [Likely] Where does ML enter: contact-reason classification, priority and urgency scoring, agent-skill matching, deflection to self-serve? [22]
    • How do you measure whether a routing model shortened resolution time and did not just move work between queues?
  5. [Likely] Airbnb's support agent was reported to be built on many models (13, per the CEO) and to resolve a large share of issues without a human [43][44]. How would you architect a multi-model support agent?
    • Which components would you expect: intent/contact-reason classifier, retriever, policy-reasoning LLM, tool/action executor, guardrail model, escalation model?
    • Which model do you route each sub-task to, and how do you balance cost, latency and quality?
  6. [Likely] Airbnb's automation platform evolved from predefined conversational workflows [26] to a version that combines LLM reasoning (chain of thought, tool selection) with those workflows plus a guardrails layer [39]. When should an action (refunds, cancellations) be handled by a deterministic workflow, and when by an LLM?
    • How do you design guardrails against off-topic, unsafe or policy-violating responses?
    • How do you handle hallucination and latency?
  7. [Likely] What is the right primary metric for an AI support agent: self-resolution rate, CSAT, reduction in contacts to humans, repeat-contact rate, or refund accuracy? How would you A/B test it?
    • Why are "share of tickets handled by AI" and "reduction in people needing a human" not the same metric? [43][44]
  8. [Likely] Design the voice support pipeline: speech-to-text, contact-reason detection, self-serve article retrieval and escalation-intent detection. [45]
    • What latency budget applies to intent detection on a live call?
    • How do you deal with transcription errors before classification?
  9. [Likely] Airbnb's earlier work fine-tuned text-generation models (for example MT5) for help-center content recommendation, agent QA assistance and chatbot paraphrasing [47]. How would you redo that system with today's LLMs, and what would you keep?
  10. [Likely] How would you fine-tune a support model (SFT, preference optimization, distillation) on millions of past agent conversations without learning bad agent behavior? [48]
    • How do you filter or weight the transcripts, and how do you protect PII in the training data?
  11. [Likely] How would you build an offline evaluation harness for a support agent, with LLM-as-judge, policy-compliance checks and human review sampling? [1]
    • How do you calibrate the LLM judge against expert agents?
  12. [Likely] A guest says the listing was not as described and asks for a refund. Walk through how the AI agent should gather evidence, apply policy and decide whether to escalate.
  13. [Likely] Design intent classification for guest-host messages (check-in questions, pet requests, cancellations, off-platform payment attempts). [22]
    • How do you discover new intents without labels, and how do you route a high-risk intent to trust and safety?

8. 2025-2026 Airbnb AI: natural-language and AI search

  1. [Likely] Airbnb has rolled out natural-language / AI-assisted search across Homes, Experiences and Services, which accepts text or voice and creates filters on the fly [49][50]. Design this system end to end.
    • How do you parse a query like "quiet cabin near a lake for 6 with a hot tub, under $300" into structured filters and soft preferences?
    • Which constraints are hard filters and which are ranking signals?
    • How do you keep classic filters and AI-generated filters consistent?
  2. [Likely] How would you combine LLM query understanding with the existing EBR and Journey Ranker stack, rather than replace it?
    • Where does free-text-to-listing matching happen: retrieval (multimodal embeddings), reranking, or both? [34]
  3. [Likely] AI search usage is opt-in and selection-biased. How would you A/B test it against classic search?
    • Which metrics separate "more engagement" from "more bookings"?
  4. [Likely] How do you prevent an LLM-powered search from claiming that listings have amenities they lack? [21]
  5. [Likely] Chesky has described an app that "doesn't just search for you; it understands you" [50]. How would you use a guest's past stays and reviews for personalization in AI search while respecting privacy?
  6. [Likely] How would you handle LLM latency and cost for search at Airbnb's query volume (caching, small models, distillation, query-class routing)?
  7. [Likely] Airbnb is reportedly building an AI agent to keep travel discovery on its own platform rather than in third-party AI assistants [51]. What ML and product risks follow from that strategy?

9. Cross-cutting senior-level probes using Airbnb context

  1. [Reported] Walk through one ML project end to end: the problem, your decisions, the trade-offs, the scope and the measured impact. Then the interviewer asks about milestones and a scope conflict. [1][4]
    • [Likely] Be ready for the interviewer to drop the project and ask an ML design question instead [5]. How would you link your project to an Airbnb problem?
  2. [Likely] Which Airbnb ML paper or blog post do you know best? What would you have done differently, and what would you try next?
  3. [Likely] Pick one Airbnb surface (search, similar listings, pricing, support). In your view, what is the current bottleneck in its ML system, and what is your 6-month roadmap?
  4. [Likely] Airbnb's published ranking gains are often small, such as about 0.3% booking conversion [29][31]. How do you decide whether an improvement that small is real and worth the complexity?
    • How do you account for long-term effects and novelty?
  5. [Likely] Airbnb inventory is seasonal, and travel demand changed sharply in 2020-2021. How would you make ranking and pricing models robust to large distribution shifts?
  6. [Likely] How do you prioritize between model improvements, feature-platform investment and experimentation-speed investment such as interleaving?
  7. [Likely] How would you design an ML system that serves Homes, Experiences and Services, which have very different inventory and booking patterns? Do you share models or separate them?
  8. [Likely] Airbnb's audience-expansion work uses extreme classification for marketing in a two-sided marketplace [32]. How would you choose which users to target for a promotional email, and how would you measure incremental impact?
  9. [Likely] Describe a time your offline evaluation and online results disagreed. How did you diagnose it, and what did you change in your process? This is the project-deep-dive version of question 6. [1]
  10. [Likely] An Airbnb ranker underperforms in a single market, such as a new country. How do you debug it? - What do you check first: feature coverage, label sparsity, currency normalization, or local inventory differences?
  11. [Likely] A host claims their listing "disappeared" from search. How would you explain the ranking change to them? - What tooling (ranking dashboards, explanations) would you build for internal teams? [11]
